Items of Discusison 
1.
Wicomico County and GMB provided an overview on the purpose and goals for
the WC-WSMP.  The Purpose statement was reviewed and is attached to these
minutes.
2.
The objective of the WC-WSMP is to evaluate areas throughout the County and
determine alternatives for providing safe drinking water and sewer to all residents
throughout the County.  During the initial phase of this study the service areas
being evaluated coincide with growth areas from the 2017 Wicomico County
Comprehensive plan Land Use Map.  This map is attached for reference.
3.
The County is meeting with all municipalities within the County to provide an
overview of the work to be completed.
4.
Town on Delmar is located partially in Maryland and partially in Delaware.  The
Maryland governing entity operates and maintains the wastewater facility.  The
water facility is located on the Delaware side of town.
5.
Existing Town of Delmar planning documents and water and sewer infrastructure
capacity were reviewed.
a.
Wastewater: Enhanced Nutrient Removal plant rated at 850,000 GPD.
b.
Water:
i.
Three (3) existing wells.
D R A F T

<!-- page 248 -->
WC-WSMP: Town of Delmar Meeting Minutes 
September 11, 2019 
Page 2 
ii.
Two (2) Elevated water towers at 250,000 and 300,000 gallon
capacity.
iii.
Water Plant rated at 600,000 gpd.
6.
Town currently has some concerns with Infiltration and Inflow (I/I) within their older
sewer systems.  The I/I have caused increased flow records at the Wastewater
facility.  Until the I/I concern is resolved Delmar could not take on additional sewer
loads.
a.
The Town noted the water production rate to serve all existing users is
around 500,000 gpd.  If the I/I was resolved they anticipate wastewater
flows to be within the same range as water production rates.
7.
The Town of Delmar has multiple Urban Service Agreements throughout the
service area and has had success with this approach. There are areas within the
middle of the municipal boundary that are still County areas but are served by
Delmar water and sewer services under an Urban Service Agreement.
a.
The property owner is responsible to cover any associated cost with
extending the water and sewer services.
8.
Town has had success with managing the water and wastewater facilities under
a Utility Commission representing both Delaware and Maryland.  Residents within
the municipal boundary of the Town are treated equally regardless of state
location.
9.
One of the Town’s challenges is staffing the wastewater facility and manpower
associated with maintenance of aging equipment.
10. The WC-WSMP will be looking at providing County wide water and sewer and the
alternatives for developing a governing entity to manage this infrastructure.  This
governing structure is not known at this time but could be County sponsored,
Regional Authority, Interjurisdictional agreements or Privately sponsored.
11. At this time the County is looking at the different service areas and would like to
develop several pilot projects.  The County could facilitate finding funding sources
for completing infrastructure projects within a certain service areas and partner
with municipalities where possible.  The project goal would be to expand water
and sewer services to unserved areas.
a.
Higher priority may be focused in areas where failing septic systems are
known.
b.
In addition, areas where developed parcels have smaller lots (less than 1
acre) and older septic systems are a concern because as a septic system
ages and fails there is no additional land area for a replacement system.
12. It was discussed as septic tanks are taken offline, associated nutrient loads could
be available to expand existing wastewater facilities or build new facilities.  In
addition, as septic tanks are taken offline the County could apply applicable
D R A F T

<!-- page 249 -->
WC-WSMP: Town of Delmar Meeting Minutes 
September 11, 2019 
Page 3 
credits to their existing MS4 permits under MDE Phase III Watershed 
Implementation Plan. 
13. Discussed the draft WC-WSMP service area for the Town of Delmar which
incorporates “Town Transition” areas as defined in the Wicomico County 2017
Comprehensive Plan – Land Use map.  A large portion of the town transition
growth area is located toward the east of the existing municipal boundary.  Current
Town planning documents do not reflect services provided to this area.
14. Concerns were expressed by the Town regarding the town transition areas and if
the WC-WSMP and/or County Comprehensive Plans would result in a financial
burden on the municipalities to serve these areas.
15. The Town of Delmar would be interested in discussing this study further as the
County moves forward.  Future meetings will be scheduled as progress continues.
Please let us know if you see the need for any corrections or revisions.  
Respectfully Submitted, 
Katherine J. McAllister, P.E. 
Executive Vice President

October 2, 2019 
9:00 A.M. 
 
 
A meeting with the Town of Sharptown was held on Wednesday, October 2, 2019, at 
9:00 a.m., at the Sharptown Town Hall.  Those in attendance were:

Items of Discusison 
 
1. Wicomico County and GMB provided an overview on the purpose and goals for the 
WC-WSMP.  The Purpose Statement was reviewed and is attached to these minutes.   
   
2. The objective of the WC-WSMP is to evaluate areas throughout the County and 
determine alternatives for providing safe drinking water and sewer to all residents 
throughout the County.  During the initial phase of this study, the service areas being 
evaluated coincide with growth areas from the 2017 Wicomico County 
Comprehensive Plan Land Use Map.  This map is attached for reference.  
 
3. The County is meeting with all municipalities within the County to provide an overview 
of the work to be completed.  The focus is on existing homes and properties.  County 
will also review areas of existing failing septic systems.   
 
4. Overall there are approximately 35,000 homes in County.  Half of those (17,500 
homes) have central water and sewer, while the other half (17500 homes) have well 
and septic.   
 
5. Town of Sharptown has worked in detail with Maryland Department of Planning 
(MDP) to develop Growth Tier maps and review future sewer and water service 
areas.  These maps have been adopted by Town.  GMB and County to review Town 
D R A F T

<!-- page 254 -->
WC-WSMP: Town of Sharptown Meeting Minutes 
October 2, 2019 
Page 2 
 
 
 
maps with County Land Use Plan when reviewing town transition/growth areas.  See 
attached maps.   
 
6. As part of the WC-WSMP, County and GMB should review adopted Growth Tier 
maps and be mindful of Tier 4 and other designated non-growth areas.   
 
7. Sharptown policy is to require annexation for any properties which they provide water 
and sewer.   
 
8. Discussed the Sam Domingo community as an example project.  If existing septic 
systems were to fail in this area, the County could partner with Sharptown to provide 
public sewer or water to this community.  This may include County funding 
infrastructure upgrades while Sharptown develops Urban Service to provide the 
public sewer or water.  The County could also consider installing a package sewer or 
water system in this area.  The goal of all future projects would be to provide a win-
win situation for all involved, while providing the necessary water/sewer utilities to 
maintain public health.   
    
9. Existing Town of Sharptown water and sewer infrastructure capacity were reviewed. 
 
a. Wastewater: Activated Sludge plant rated at 150,000 GPD.  
b. Water: 
i. 
Four (4) existing wells.  Two (2) deep wells and two (2) shallow wells.  Blend 
three (3) at a time.  
ii. Elevated storage capacity of 200,000 gallons.   
iii. Water system only required disinfection.   
    
10. The WC-WSMP will be looking at providing County wide water and sewer and the 
alternatives for developing a governing entity to manage this infrastructure.  This 
governing structure is not known at this time but could be County sponsored, 
Regional Authority, Interjurisdictional agreements, or Privately sponsored.   
 
11. At this time the County is looking at the different service areas and would like to 
develop several pilot projects.  The County could facilitate finding funding sources for 
completing infrastructure projects within certain service areas and partner with 
municipalities where possible.  The project goal would be to expand water and sewer 
services to unserved areas.   
 
a. Higher priority may be focused in areas where failing septic systems are known.   
b. In addition, areas where developed parcels have smaller lots (less than 1 acre) 
and older septic systems are a concern because as a septic system ages and 
fails, there is no additional land area for a replacement system.   
 
12. It was discussed as septic tanks are taken offline, associated nutrient loads could be 
available to expand existing wastewater facilities or build new facilities.  In addition, 
as septic tanks are taken offline, the County could apply applicable credits to their 
existing MS4 permits under MDE Phase III Watershed Implementation Plan. 
D R A F T

<!-- page 255 -->
WC-WSMP: Town of Sharptown Meeting Minutes 
October 2, 2019 
Page 3 
 
 
 
   
13. Concerns were expressed by the Town regarding the WC-WSMP and requirements 
or financial burdens this may place on Town.   County noted the goal is for this to be 
a technical study and only serve as a roadmap for the County to serve unserved 
areas as needed.    
    
14. The Town of Sharptown would be interested in discussing this study further as the 
County moves forward.  Future meetings will be scheduled as progress continues.   
    
Please let us know if you see the need for any corrections or revisions.   
 
 
 
Respectfully Submitted, 
 
 
 
Katherine J. McAllister, P.E. 
Executive Vice President

Items of Discusison 
 
1. Wicomico County and GMB provided an overview on the purpose and goals for 
the WC-WSMP.  The Purpose Statement was reviewed and is attached to these 
minutes.   
   
2. The objective of the WC-WSMP is to evaluate areas throughout the County and 
determine alternatives for providing safe drinking water and sewer to all residents 
throughout the County.  During the initial phase of this study, the service areas 
being evaluated coincide with growth areas from the 2017 Wicomico County 
Comprehensive Plan Land Use Map.  This map is attached for reference.  
 
3. The County is meeting with all municipalities within the County to provide an 
overview of the work to be completed.  The focus is on existing homes and 
properties.  County will also review areas of existing failing septic systems.   
 
4. Overall there are approximately 35,000 homes in County.  Half of those (17,500 
homes) have central water and sewer, while the other half (17500 homes) have 
well and septic.   
 
5. Pittsville requires annexation if they serve any property with public water and 
sewer.  The current focus is to develop within the Town boundaries and 
designated growth areas.  
D R A F T

<!-- page 262 -->
WC-WSMP: Town of Pittsville Meeting Minutes 
November 27, 2019 
Page 2 
 
 
 
6. Town of Pittsville discussed the existing wastewater facility.  Currently at 90-95% 
capacity.  There is acreage (approximately 25 acres) at the facility to expand.  
Currently working with MDE on future upgrade plans.  
 
7. Discussed the Parsonsburg area as an example project and the fact there is no 
central water or sewer available in this community.  As a potential future project, 
the County could partner with Pittsville to provide public sewer or water to this 
community.  This may include County funding infrastructure upgrades while 
Pittsville develops Urban Service to provide the public sewer or water.  Pittsville 
would need to review in detail, but this may be an alternative if it benefits both 
Town of Pittsville and Parsonsburg.    
 
8. The County is also considering installing package sewer or water systems in 
unserved areas.  The goal of all future projects would be to provide a win-win 
situation for all involved while providing the necessary water/sewer utilities to 
maintain public health.   
    
9. Discussed concerns with being a small municipal community and the need to 
maintain user rates that can sustain the costs associated with operating and 
maintaining the facilities while also keeping it affordable for community.  Smaller 
municipalities struggle with the economy of scale due to small number of users 
but high operation and maintenance cost.   
 
10. Discussed that funding with USDA and other government agencies for water and 
sewer infrastructure require rates to be higher to qualify for grant funds, but the 
increased rates are not affordable for residents.  
 
11. Small municipalities struggle with handling and disposing of sludge.  A regional 
sludge facility for Wicomico County communities would assist in facility issues and 
costs.  
 
12. Pittsville is currently working on an update to the Town Comprehensive Plan.  This 
will be available in February and should provide more detailed maps of planned 
growth areas.    
 
13. Reviewed Town of Pittsville existing water and sewer infrastructure capacity. 
 
a. Wastewater: Activated Sludge plant rated at 115,000 gpd. 
b.  Water: 
i. 
Two (2) active wells.   
ii. Water Plant capacity is 134,000 gpd. 
iii. Elevated storage capacity of 190,000 gallons.   
    
14. The WC-WSMP will be looking at providing County wide water and sewer and the 
alternatives for developing a governing entity to manage this infrastructure.  This 
D R A F T

governing structure is not known at this time but could be County sponsored, 
Regional Authority, Interjurisdictional agreements, or Privately sponsored.   
 
15. At this time the County is looking at the different service areas and would like to 
develop several pilot projects.  The County could facilitate finding funding sources 
for completing infrastructure projects within certain service areas and partner with 
municipalities where possible.  The project goal would be to expand water and 
sewer services to unserved areas. 
   
a. Higher priority may be focused in areas where failing septic systems are 
known.   
b. In addition, areas where developed parcels have smaller lots (less than 1 acre) 
and older septic systems are a concern because as a septic system ages and 
fails, there is no additional land area for a replacement system.   
 
16. It was discussed as septic tanks are taken offline, associated nutrient loads could

Items of Discusison 
 
1. Wicomico County and GMB provided an overview on the purpose and goals for the 
WC-WSMP.  The Purpose Statement was reviewed and is attached to these minutes.   
   
2. The objective of the WC-WSMP is to evaluate areas throughout the County and 
determine alternatives for providing safe drinking water and sewer to all residents 
throughout the County.  During the initial phase of this study, the service areas being 
evaluated coincide with growth areas from the 2017 Wicomico County 
Comprehensive Plan Land Use Map.  This map is attached for reference.  
 
3. The County is meeting with all municipalities within the County to provide an overview 
of the work to be completed.  The focus is on existing homes and properties.  County 
will also review areas of existing failing septic systems.   
 
4. Overall there are approximately 35,000 homes in County.  Half of those (17,500 
homes) have central water and sewer, while the other half (17,500 homes) have well 
and septic.   
 
5. City of Salisbury noted by code, any property to be served with City public water and 
sewer is required to either annex or sign a pre-annexation agreement.   
 
D R A F T

<!-- page 266 -->
WC-WSMP: City of Salisbury Meeting Minutes 
December 3, 2019 
Page 2 
 
 
 
6. At this time the City has been responding to development request and extending 
water and sewer service as requested.  Typically, Developer pays for infrastructure 
to extend water and sewer.  
 
7. Current City policy require all areas that receive public sewer must also have public 
water services.  Reviewed ordinance under City code. 
 
8. Reviewed unserved and unannexed parcels that are surrounded by City on most 
sides.  Ultimately if public health concerns rose, these areas would be served public 
water and sewer by City.  In most cases, the areas will be required to annex.  City 
does not want to see pockets of areas not served by public utilities.    
 
9. Discussed if some of the lower income unannexed areas could receive public water 
and sewer under urban services.  Areas with small lots and failing septic are a 
concern as they become abandoned.  For lower income neighbors, the affordability 
of annexation and paying for infrastructure is not always feasible in some areas.   
 
10. Under an urban service agreement, the City would not be required to provide 
additional City services such as trash, sidewalks, lighting, etc.  A project may include 
the County funding infrastructure upgrades while City of Salisbury develops urban 
service agreement to provide the public sewer or water.  This is not standard practice 
for the City and would require City Council review on a case-by-case basis.  Could 
also consider agreements which allow property taxes to be staggered.   
 
11. The goal of all future projects would be to provide a win-win situation for all involved, 
while providing the necessary water/sewer utilities to maintain public health.   
 
12. The City does not foresee expanding its growth boundaries north toward Dagsboro 
Road.   
 
13. The City Comprehensive Plan will be updated in 2022.   
    
14. Reviewed City of Salisbury existing water and sewer infrastructure capacity. 
 
a. Wastewater: Enhanced Nutrient Removal plant rated at 8.5 MGD.  
b. Water: 
i. 
Seven (7) active wells at Park Plant.  Two (2) wells at Paleo Plant with third 
(3rd) well going in construction soon.   
ii. Water Plant capacity at Park is 5.7 MGD.  Paleo plant capacity is 6.3 MGD. 
iii. Elevated and ground storage capacity of 4.5 MGD.   
    
15. The WC-WSMP will be looking at providing County wide water and sewer and the 
alternatives for developing a governing entity to manage this infrastructure.  This 
governing structure is not known at this time but could be County sponsored, 
Regional Authority, Interjurisdictional agreements or Privately sponsored.   
 
D R A F T

<!-- page 267 -->
WC-WSMP: City of Salisbury Meeting Minutes 
December 3, 2019 
Page 3 
 
 
 
16. At this time the County is looking at the different service areas and would like to 
develop several pilot projects.  The County could facilitate finding funding sources for 
completing infrastructure projects within certain service areas and partner with 
municipalities where possible.  The project goal would be to expand water and sewer 
services to unserved areas.  
  
a. Higher priority may be focused in areas where failing septic systems are known.   
b. In addition, areas where developed parcels have smaller lots (less than 1 acre) 
and older septic systems are a concern because as a septic system ages and 
fails, there is no additional land area for a replacement system.   
 
17. County should note funding opportunities will be limited in areas not part of a Priority 
Funding Area (PFA).   Most funding will hinge on area being part of PFA. 
 
18. It was discussed as septic tanks are taken offline, associated nutrient loads could be 
available to expand existing wastewater facilities or build new facilities.  In addition, 
as septic tanks are taken offline, the County could apply applicable credits to their 
existing MS4 permits or the City of Salisbury MS4 permit under MDE Phase III 
Watershed Implementation Plan. 
      
19. City of Salisbury would be interested in discussing this study further as the County 
moves forward.  Future meetings will be scheduled as progress continues.   
    
Please let us know if you see the need for any corrections or revisions.   
 
 
 
Respectfully Submitted,

The objective of the WC-WSMP is to evaluate areas throughout the County and
2.
WC-WSMP.  The Purpose statement was reviewed and is attached to these minutes.
Wicomico County and GMB provided an overview on the purpose and goals for the
1.
Items of Discusison
D R A F T

<!-- page 270 -->
WC-WSMP: Town of Hebron Meeting Minutes 
December 11, 2019 
Page 2 
 
 
 
6. Water and sewer rates are billed on front footage and fixture counts.  The Town 
currently does not have any water meters.   
 
7. Hebron’s policy is to require annexation for any properties which they provide water 
and sewer.  Need to maintain tax basis to cover operation cost of utilities.   
 
8. The Town’s main focus at this time is on growth within its existing boundaries.  Policy 
is for growth to pay for growth.      
 
9. Westside Elementary school is not within Town boundary, but the Town provides 
public water and sewer.  
 
10. County discussed potential project scenarios that could include a partnership 
between the Town and County.  For example, combining Mardela Springs with 
Hebron for water and sewer utilities.  Or looking at pumping Quantico to Hebron for 
sewer treatment.  This may include County funding infrastructure upgrades while 
Hebron develops Urban Service to provide the public sewer or water.  The County is 
also considering installing a package sewer or water system in developed areas 
without public water and sewer.  The goal of all future projects would be to provide a 
win-win situation for all involved while providing the necessary water/sewer utilities 
to maintain public health.   
 
11. Discussed economic aspects of providing dependable public water and sewer which 
in turn brings increased economic growth.   
    
12. Existing Town of Hebron water and sewer infrastructure capacity were reviewed. 
 
a. Wastewater: Lagoon plant rated at 120,000 gpd.  Currently running at 85,000 
gpd.   
b. Water: 
i. Two (2) existing wells.  Water system only required disinfection.    
ii. Elevated storage capacity of 300,000 gallons.    
    
13. The WC-WSMP will be looking at providing County wide water and sewer and the 
alternatives for developing a governing entity to manage this infrastructure.  This 
governing structure is not known at this time but could be County sponsored, 
Regional Authority, Interjurisdictional agreements, or Privately sponsored.   
 
14. At this time, the County is looking at the different service areas and would like to 
develop several pilot projects.  The County could facilitate finding funding sources for 
completing infrastructure projects within certain service areas and partner with 
municipalities where possible.  The project goal would be to expand water and sewer 
services to unserved areas.   
 
a. Higher priority may be focused in areas where failing septic systems are known.   
D R A F T

<!-- page 271 -->
WC-WSMP: Town of Hebron Meeting Minutes 
December 11, 2019 
Page 3 
 
 
 
b. In addition, areas where developed parcels have smaller lots (less than 1 acre) 
and older septic systems are a concern because as a septic system ages and 
fails, there is no additional land area for a replacement system.   
 
15. It was discussed as septic tanks are taken offline, associated nutrient loads could be 
available to expand existing wastewater facilities or build new facilities.  In addition, 
as septic tanks are taken offline, the County could apply applicable credits to their 
existing MS4 permits under MDE Phase III Watershed Implementation Plan. 
      
16. The Town of Hebron would be interested in discussing this study further as the 
County moves forward.  Future meetings will be scheduled as progress continues.   
    
Please let us know if you see the need for any corrections or revisions.   
 
 
 
Respectfully Submitted, 
 
 
 
Katherine J. McAllister, P.E. 
Executive Vice President

<!-- page 273 -->
WICOMICO COUNTY 
 WATER & SEWER MASTER PLAN (WC-WSMP) 
 
City of Fruitland Meeting 
GMB File No. 190079 
 
December 12, 2019 
8:30 A.M. 
 
 
A meeting with the City of Fruitland was held on Thursday, December 12, 2019, at 8:30 
a.m., at George, Miles and Buhr LLC (GMB).  Those in attendance were: 
 
Name 
Organization 
 Email 
Dodie Kerr 
City of Fruitland 
   dkerr@cityoffruitland.com  
John Psota 
City of Fruitland 
   jpsota@cityoffruitland.com  
Mike Gibbons 
City of Fruitland 
 mgibbons@cityoffruitland.com  
Dallas Baker Jr., P.E. 
Wicomico County 
 dbaker@wicomicocounty.org  
Peter Bozick Jr., P.E. 
GMB 
 pbozick@gmbnet.com 
Katherine McAllister, P.E. GMB 
 kmcallister@gmbnet.com 
 
Items of Discusison 
 
1. Wicomico County and GMB provided an overview on the purpose and goals for the 
WC-WSMP.  The Purpose Statement was reviewed and is attached to these minutes.  
   
2. The objective of the WC-WSMP is to evaluate areas throughout the County and 
determine alternatives for providing safe drinking water and sewer to all residents 
throughout the County.  During the initial phase of this study, the service areas being 
evaluated coincide with growth areas from the 2017 Wicomico County 
Comprehensive Plan Land Use Map.  This map is attached for reference.  
 
3. The County is meeting with all municipalities within the County to provide an overview 
of the work to be completed.  The focus is on existing homes and properties.  County 
will also review areas of existing failing septic systems.   
 
4. Overall there are approximately 35,000 homes in County.  Half of those (17,500 
homes) have central water and sewer, while the other half (17500 homes) have well 
and septic.   
 
5. City of Fruitland has worked with Maryland Department of Planning (MDP) and has 
up-to-date growth planning maps.  Growth areas are located toward the southwest 
area of Fruitland.  
 
D R A F T

<!-- page 274 -->
WC-WSMP: City of Fruitland Meeting Minutes 
December 12, 2019 
Page 2 
 
 
 
6. City of Fruitland policy is to require annexation for any properties which they provide 
water and sewer.  They want to “reserve the right” to annex any property they provide 
public water and sewer.   
 
7. Discussed if lower income unannexed areas, such as nearby mobile home parks, 
could receive public water and sewer under urban services.  Areas with small lots 
and failing septic are a concern as they become abandoned.  For lower income 
neighbors, the affordability of annexation and paying for infrastructure is not always 
feasible in some areas.  County could partner with City to help fund infrastructure.  
This would need to be discussed in detail with City Council and would be based on a 
case-by-case basis.    
 
8. The goal of all future projects would be to provide a win-win situation for all involved 
while providing the necessary water/sewer utilities to maintain public health.   
    
9. Existing City of Fruitland water and sewer infrastructure capacity were reviewed. 
 
a. Wastewater: Enhanced Nutrient Removal plant rated at 1.0 MGD.  On paper, 
plant is close to capacity but have approximately 200,000 gpd additional capacity. 
b. Water: 
i. Four (4) existing wells.  One (1) new well to be under construction soon.   
ii. Elevated storage capacity, two (2) tanks, at total volume of 1.0 MGD.   
iii. Water plant capacity at 1.5 MGD.   
    
10. The WC-WSMP will be looking at providing County wide water and sewer and the 
alternatives for developing a governing entity to manage this infrastructure.  This 
governing structure is not known at this time but could be County sponsored, 
Regional Authority, Interjurisdictional agreements, or Privately sponsored.   
 
11. At this time, the County is looking at the different service areas and would like to 
develop several pilot projects.  The County could facilitate finding funding sources for 
completing infrastructure projects within a certain service areas and partner with 
municipalities where possible.  The project goal would be to expand water and sewer 
services to unserved areas.  
  
a. Higher priority may be focused in areas where failing septic systems are known.   
b. In addition, areas where developed parcels have smaller lots (less than 1 acre) 
and older septic systems are a concern because as a septic system ages and 
fails there is no additional land area for a replacement system.   
 
12. It was discussed as septic tanks are taken offline, associated nutrient loads could be 
available to expand existing wastewater facilities or build new facilities.  In addition, 
as septic tanks are taken offline the County and/or City of Fruitland could apply 
applicable credits to their existing MS4 permits under MDE Phase III Watershed 
Implementation Plan. 
      
D R A F T

<!-- page 275 -->
WC-WSMP: City of Fruitland Meeting Minutes 
December 12, 2019 
Page 3 
 
 
 
13. City of Fruitland would be interested in discussing this study further as the County 
moves forward.  Future meetings will be scheduled as progress continues.   
    
Please let us know if you see the need for any corrections or revisions.   
 
 
 
Respectfully Submitted,
